I feel like we're going in circles here. It kind of reminds me of when customers ask the exact same question three times in a row. Like yesterday, this guy asked me if we sell stamps, and I told him no, and then two minutes later he came back to my register and asked again. \n\nWe haven't sold stamps in like a year because my manager, Craig, got into this massive argument with the mail carrier. Craig is so incredibly petty, he actually refused to sign for a delivery just to prove a point, so now whoever is on the morning shift has to drive all the store's outgoing mail to the post office ourselves. It's so stupid. The post office is right next to this bakery that makes these giant cinnamon rolls, though, which is pretty sweet. They're like the size of my head, but you have to get there before 10 AM or they're totally sold out. My coworker brought some in once and we ate them in the breakroom, which honestly was a huge mistake because they are so sticky and our breakroom sink has been clogged for a week. Someone dumped coffee grounds down there and maintenance still hasn't come to fix it. \n\nBut um, anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, like I just said a second ago, I guess it's possible it's not as noticeable to them? I just really don't know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the spot on your face doesn't actually exist?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's definitely there. I mean, I see it every single day. Like, how could it not exist if I'm looking right at it? It's kind of like... well, it's just there. Kind of like how Craig is always just *there* hovering around the registers when we're trying to close out. It's like they were specifically designed to wash you out and show every single flaw. \n\nActually, the lights in aisle four have been flickering for like a month, and it literally gives me a migraine every time I have to restock the canned soup. I told Craig about it, and he just wrote it down on his stupid clipboard and did nothing. He said maintenance is backed up because someone broke the automatic doors at the entrance. A customer rammed their shopping cart right into the glass because they weren't paying attention. They were looking at their phone, probably playing Candy Crush or something. My mom is obsessed with that game, she literally sends me lives on Facebook every single day, even though I haven't played it since high school. \n\nBut um, anyway... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the spot on my face. Yeah, even when other people, like my mom or Sarah, say they don't see it, it just feels like it's the very first thing customers and managers notice when they look at me. So it's hard to believe it's just in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the spot on your face is only visible to you and not to anyone else?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know. Like, maybe? I guess it could just be me, but it's hard to tell because people don't always say what they're thinking, you know? Like, they could just be being polite. People are so fake sometimes. Like this one customer yesterday, she was smiling at me and being all sweet, but then she threw a fit because her coupons were expired. She had this whole binder of them, like those extreme couponers on TLC? I used to watch that show with my sister when we were younger. We'd sit on the couch and eat popcorn, and she always put way too much butter on it. Now she's super into health food and only eats kale and quinoa. She tried to make me this kale smoothie once and it literally tasted like dirt. I had to spit it out in the sink when she wasn't looking. \n\nBut um... wait, sorry, what did you ask me again? Oh, if the spot is only visible to me. I don't know, I just... I don't really have an answer for that. I mean, I see it, so it's hard to believe nobody else does.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the spot on your face is something you imagine and not actually there?\n\nPatient: Um, imagine it? I mean, I guess I've wondered if maybe I'm making it a bigger deal than it is, but... not actually there? That's hard to wrap my head around. Like, how could I just make up a physical thing on my face? It's like when people say they see ghosts or something. My aunt Brenda swears her house is haunted. She says she hears footsteps in the hallway at night and that her cat, Mr. Whiskers, stares at empty corners and hisses. She actually bought all this sage and went around burning it in every room, and it made the whole house smell like a campfire. It got into the curtains and everything. I couldn't even go over there for Thanksgiving last year because the smell was giving me such a bad headache, so I just stayed home and ordered Chinese food. Which was actually fine, because the delivery guy was really fast, though he forgot my egg rolls. It's so annoying when you pay for something and they just leave it out of the bag. Like, how hard is it to check a receipt? \n\nBut um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, if I'm just imagining the spot. I don't know. It feels really real to me when I look in the mirror, so I don't think I'm just making it up entirely. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the spot on your face is something that only exists in your mind?\n\nPatient: In my mind? Um... I feel like we're kind of going in circles here, but I really just don't know. Like, the mind is a weird thing, right? I was actually just listening to this podcast about how people can convince themselves of crazy things. My coworker Jessica recommended it to me. She literally wears her AirPods her entire shift and hides them under her hair so she can listen to stuff while she rings people up. It's totally against store policy, but Dave—he's our shift lead—never writes her up because they go to the same gym. He is so strict about the rules with everyone else, though. Yesterday I was literally one minute late coming back from my fifteen-minute break because the vending machine in the back ate my dollar bill. It just sucked it in and wouldn't give me my Diet Coke or my money back. I tried shaking the machine a little bit, and Dave came speed-walking around the corner like I was destroying company property. He made me feel like a criminal over a stupid dollar. \n\nBut um... wait, sorry, what were you saying? Oh, about it being in my mind. Yeah, I just... I don't know. It's hard to believe it's one hundred percent in my head when I can literally point to the spot in the mirror. So I really can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the spot on your face is not something that can be seen by anyone else, including doctors or dermatologists, if they were to examine your face?\n\nPatient: Doctors or dermatologists? I mean, maybe? But that's actually the whole reason I came in here. I just keep getting stuck on this tiny uneven spot around my nose and cheek. I see it all the time, especially in photos and under those horrible fluorescent store lights at work. They are so ridiculously bright. My manager, Dave, refuses to change this one bulb above register three that constantly flickers, and it literally gives me such a migraine by the end of my shift. My friend Chloe says fluorescent lights age you faster, which is why she buys all these expensive serums from Sephora now. We went there last weekend and she spent like eighty dollars on this tiny little glass dropper bottle of vitamin C stuff. It smelled exactly like hot dog water, I swear. I don't know how she puts that on her face every morning. I'd rather just use my regular drugstore moisturizer. Oh, and then we went to get iced coffees after and the barista spelled my name wrong again, which happens all the time, but it's like, how hard is it to just ask how to spell it? \n\nBut um... wait, what did you just ask me? Oh, about doctors seeing the spot. I don't know. Because even when other people tell me they don't see it, it honestly feels like it's the very first thing the customers and managers notice when they walk up to my register. So if a dermatologist actually examined my face, I feel like they'd have to see it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking a professional medical opinion, such as from a dermatologist, to assess the spot on your face?\n\nPatient: A dermatologist? Um, I haven't really thought about it, honestly. I don't even know if my insurance from the store would cover something like that. They gave us this huge packet about our benefits during orientation, and it was literally as thick as a dictionary. My coworker Jessica tried to use her insurance for a dentist appointment last month because she chipped her front tooth on a frozen Uncrustable. She actually eats them straight out of the freezer, which is so weird to me because the peanut butter gets all hard and crumbly. But anyway, she was on hold with the insurance company for like forty-five minutes while we were in the breakroom. The hold music was this awful jazz saxophone that was so loud I couldn't even hear the video I was trying to watch on my phone. And then when she finally got through, the lady on the phone told her she wasn't even covered for dental until she'd been working there for a full year. So she just had to walk around with a jagged tooth for weeks, and Dave kept giving her these weird looks every time she smiled at a customer. \n\nBut um... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, going to a skin doctor. Yeah, no, I haven't really looked into that. I just mostly focus on it in my own bathroom mirror, so I haven't tried to make an appointment or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think a dermatologist would be able to see the spot on your face if they examined you?\n\nPatient: Um, would a dermatologist see it? I mean, I don't know, maybe? I feel like we just talked about this, didn't we? It's so confusing to think about. Kind of like how confusing the skincare aisle is at Target. I was there on Tuesday after my shift because I needed to buy more cotton rounds, and they completely rearranged the whole section. They moved all the acne stuff to where the body wash used to be, and I spent like twenty minutes just wandering around looking for my usual brand. And of course, the one time I actually need help, there's not a single employee around. But when I'm just browsing and want to be left alone, they ask me if I need help finding anything like five times. It's so annoying. Anyway, I ended up buying this new micellar water that was on sale, but the bottle leaked in my bag on the way home and got all over my favorite scrunchie. It's this velvet pink one that Chloe got me for my birthday, and now it smells like weird cucumber chemicals. \n\nWait, what did you ask me again? Oh, about the dermatologist seeing the spot. Yeah, I really don't know. I guess I'm just not sure what they would or wouldn't see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that a dermatologist would not find anything unusual about the spot on your face if they were to examine it?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe they wouldn't find anything? It's kind of hard to say. I guess it's possible they might just brush it off. Doctors are always in such a rush anyway. Like when I went to the urgent care last winter for this horrible cough, the doctor was in the room for literally two minutes. He didn't even sit down! He just stood by the door with his tablet and then prescribed me this cough syrup that tasted exactly like black licorice. I hate black licorice so much. My grandma used to always keep those Good & Plenty candies in this crystal bowl on her coffee table, and she would try to give them to me every time we visited. They always got stuck in my teeth, and the bowl itself was always kind of sticky, which is gross to think about now. I don't know why she didn't just buy normal candy like M&Ms or something. \n\nBut um... sorry, wait. What was the question? Oh, about the dermatologist not finding anything. Yeah, I guess it's totally possible they wouldn't think it was a big deal or unusual. I just don't know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that a dermatologist would tell you there's nothing wrong with the spot on your face?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess they could say there's nothing wrong with it. But honestly, doctors can be so dismissive sometimes. Like my friend Sarah went to a doctor because she was having these terrible migraines, right? And the doctor just told her she was probably stressed from work and told her to drink more water. Drink more water! Like she hasn't tried that. We work at the same store, and she always has this giant Stanley cup with her at the register. It's one of those really heavy ones, the pale pink color that was sold out everywhere for like three months. She actually paid double for it on eBay because she wanted it so bad. I could never do that. I just use this plastic water bottle I got for free at a 5K I didn't even run in. My mom made me go with her to hand out bananas at the finish line, and it was freezing that morning. \n\nBut um, wait, sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, right, the dermatologist. Yeah, I mean, it's possible they'd say nothing is wrong. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the spot on your face is only a product of your imagination and not based on any actual physical characteristic?\n\nPatient: Um, just my imagination? I... I don't know. I guess maybe it could be? But it feels so real when I look in the mirror. It's like when you stare at a word for too long and it stops looking like a real word, you know? Like the word \"queue.\" Q-U-E-U-E. It has way too many vowels. I was trying to spell it the other day when I was texting my manager about the line at the register being too long. We were totally understaffed because Jason called out sick again. He always calls out on Saturdays when it's the busiest. I swear he just wants to go to those car meets downtown. He's always posting pictures of his Honda Civic on Instagram, and he put this massive spoiler on the back that looks completely ridiculous. It's not even a fast car! And the exhaust is so loud, you can hear him pull into the employee parking lot from inside the breakroom. \n\nWait, sorry, I'm rambling again. What did you ask me? Oh, if the spot is just in my head. I mean, I guess it's possible it's just my imagination playing tricks on me. I really don't know anymore."
